PANW — Evidence appendix
All harvested proof points · company first, then peers

Evidence

Claims by section
business-model
  • Palo Alto Networks sells enterprise security in three commercial forms, all of them subscriptions attached to something already deployed.
  • Management gives the software share of trailing product revenue separately: 22% at Q3 FY2023, 44% at Q1 FY2026 and 46% at Q3 FY2026 (Q3 FY2026 earnings call).
  • Management's 70% ties the estimate to disclosure, and it describes a quarter containing only 2.6 months of CyberArk: 70% of Q3 FY2026's $3,002m is $2,101m against organic revenue of $2,614m, or 80.4%.
  • At the disclosed attach of more than four subscriptions per installed device, the annual recurring bill at list is 4 x 27% + 19% = 127% of the box price, so across a five-year deployment the customer pays roughly 6.4x the appliance price in subscriptions and support and the box is about 14% of five-year spend.
  • Non-cancelable purchase commitments at 2026-04-30 were $8,092m for cloud hosting against $246m for manufacturing, and the whole manufacturing commitment falls inside FY2027 (Q3 FY2026 10-Q, Note 11).
  • Marginal cloud hosting cost per incremental dollar of NGS ARR went from 9.1% in FY2024 to 14.0% in FY2025 to 18.6% across nine months of FY2026.
competitive-advantage
  • The Network Security advantage is a real installed-base annuity. It defends PANW's own revenue well and it converts into neither an excess margin nor compounding share.
  • Among the roughly 2,280 platformized accounts, net retention is 120% with single-digit churn.
  • FY2025 gross margin of 73.4% was 7.1 points below Fortinet's 80.5% and 13.3 points below Check Point's 86.7%, and GAAP operating margin of 13.5% was 17.2 points below Fortinet's 30.7%.
  • Gross margin in FY2025 matched FY2016's 73.2% on 6.7x the revenue, so a decade of volume produced no unit-cost leverage.
  • The whole gap is in the recurring line, where PANW earned 72.5% against Fortinet's disclosed 86.8% service gross margin.
  • PANW's product gross margin exceeds its subscription and support gross margin, 77.1% against 72.5% in FY2025 and 71.9% against 66.5% in Q3 FY2026.
  • Cortex grew from a small base to roughly a fifth of the company over the same span; adjusted for that, PANW's network-security-only share of the pool was between 1.4 points lower and 1.1 points higher in 2025 than in 2020.
  • In the two most recent comparable quarters the refresh went at least as much to the peer: Fortinet's product revenue grew 52% year over year to $773m in the June-2026 quarter against PANW's 31% to $594m in the April-2026 quarter, and the PANW figure includes CyberArk software licences.
  • Gartner's 2026 SASE Platforms Magic Quadrant, reported 2026-08-03, placed PANW third behind Netskope and Cato, down from first
  • This advantage earns its keep today, is defensible for now, and is open to erosion.
  • Cortex earns high returns on each incremental sale and has not returned its cost of capital on the money that built it. The asset producing the incremental sale belongs to Network Security.
  • A 20% operating margin, above Dynatrace's 12.2% at $2.0bn of revenue and above Datadog's −1.3% at $3.4bn, produces $340m-$400m of after-tax profit at PANW's FY2025 effective rate of 28.9%, which is 4.6%-5.6% on the purchase price before any organic research spending, against a hurdle near 9-10% for a company financed almost entirely with equity.
  • The durability test has run once and Cortex lost it. Cloud security was the first leg to meet a well-funded specialist.
  • Combined Cortex and Cloud ARR grew "nearly 25%" year over year in Q4 FY2025 while XSIAM roughly doubled over the same twelve months, which leaves the rest of Cortex — XDR, XSOAR, Xpanse and the cloud portfolio — growing about 10% on a base near $1.4bn
  • And the average XSIAM customer is getting smaller: above $1.0m of ARR at roughly 400 customers in Q4 FY2025, "nearly $1 million" at more than 600 in Q2 FY2026, and about $811k across 740 in Q3 FY2026.
  • Cortex earns excess returns on the incremental sale today and is defensible for the twelve to eighteen months a security-operations deployment takes to unwind, and it is open to erosion by three attackers who reach the same buyer through a channel of comparable strength.
  • The identity franchise holds a real switching cost at the level of the installed credential vault, and that switching cost is the part of the moat supported without qualification.
  • PANW assigned $3,500m of the $21,061m purchase price - 56% of all identified intangibles - to platform renewals amortised over 12 to 14 years, against $219m for the in-place contract backlog over two years (Q3 FY2026 10-Q, Note 7).
  • Adding back all $115.8m of amortisation of acquired intangibles but still charging the $234.4m of share-based compensation, CyberArk's 2025 operating margin was -1.1% on $1,361.1m of revenue.
  • Microsoft lists Entra ID P2, which includes Privileged Identity Management, at $10.00 per user per month and includes it at no incremental charge with Microsoft 365 E5
  • It is not in the class of positions that have survived well-funded attempts to break them: Microsoft, Okta and SailPoint are all attacking it now, and the attack has already compressed the economics to the point where a category leader runs at breakeven.
  • On capital net of float, invested capital was negative in eight of the ten years and minus $2,641m in FY2025: total deferred revenue of $12,751.9m is 26% larger than the $10,111.3m base it funds.
  • Free cash flow less share-based compensation is 23.6% of revenue at PANW, 28.4% at Fortinet and 35.5% at Check Point, so PANW is the least cash-generative of the three network security incumbents on that basis.
competitive-landscape
  • Dell'Oro's last published vendor split for the firewall segment covers 2Q 2024: combined physical and virtual firewall revenue of $3.2bn, up 1.5%, split $2.8bn physical (down 1%) and $420m virtual (up 22%), with PANW at 29% and Cisco and Fortinet at 15% each
  • Fortinet therefore earns 15% of firewall revenue on 55% of units, about 0.27x the market average revenue per unit.
  • Over the decade Check Point gave up 26.2 points of the pool. PANW took 12.3, Zscaler 10.7 and Fortinet 3.2.
  • At those rates Zscaler adds roughly $710-880m of ARR a year against PANW's $640m, so the dollar gap widens from about $1.9bn to about $2.2bn over the next twelve months.
  • In cloud security PANW went from a book larger than Wiz's in mid-2024, above $700m of ARR against roughly $500m, to third.
  • PAM is roughly 11% of the $29bn PANW cites, so 89% of the market it says it entered is served by products Idira either does not lead in or has only just acquired.
  • SailPoint is closing the gap: CyberArk's ARR was 1.33x SailPoint's at the December-2024 and January-2025 dates and 1.28x a year later, and SailPoint's SaaS ARR grew 38.1% against CyberArk's 30% subscription-ARR growth, at the same revenue per employee.
critical-points
  • Attached subscriptions and support is about $4,926m, 43% of FY2026 revenue, and it grew roughly 7% in FY2026.
  • PANW spends 21.5% of revenue on research and development against Fortinet's 12.0% and Check Point's 16.8%, and it absorbs the delivery cost of running security from its own cloud, worth about 14 points of gross margin on 80% of revenue.
expectations
  • Two of the five scoreable targets were met only because CyberArk and Chronosphere arrived inside the target window.
  • Q4 FY2026 therefore needs 140 to 250 basis points of sequential margin improvement in the first quarter that includes a full period of CyberArk, plus Portkey from 2026-05-29, against a Q3 that included only 2.6 months of CyberArk and still gave up roughly 300 basis points sequentially.
financial-history
  • Across FY2020 to FY2025 the 18.8 points of GAAP operating-margin expansion split as +2.7 from gross margin, +1.0 from research and development, +11.1 from sales and marketing and +4.0 from general and administrative.
  • Four-fifths of the FY2025 GAAP operating-margin expansion came from items that will not repeat in the same form.
  • FY2025 free cash flow of $3,469.8m was 37.6% of revenue against a 13.5% GAAP operating margin, and the difference is $1,295.1m of share-based compensation, $343.4m of depreciation and amortisation, and growth in deferred revenue.
forward-model
  • And Network Security is 75% of revenue today and 59% by FY2035 in the base case, with Cortex at 26% and Identity at 15%.
  • The base case reaches $17.1bn without further acquisitions.
history
  • Growth targets are beaten, margin targets are missed on the stated date and reached one to two years late, and long-dated aspirational targets are replaced by larger ones before the original can be tested.
management-claims
  • The growth claim is supported: SASE ARR of $1.6bn growing 40% against a Dell'Oro SASE market growing 21% in 1Q 2026, with nearly 50 displacement wins worth about $200m of contract value in nine months.
  • Dividing the slides by each other gives $47 per user per year at the target against CyberArk's realised $158 of subscription ARR per user today, so blended realised price per identity has to decline about 70% for the volume expansion to fit inside the market management cites.
  • Hardware is about 10% of revenue, so a 40% increase on a roughly $215m year-ago quarterly base adds about $85m, and the price rise caps how much of the 40% can be volume at about 30 points.
secular-trends
  • The binding quantity for roughly 80% of PANW revenue is the enterprise security budget.
  • Underlying growth has held in a 14-16% band for seven consecutive quarters. The step to 31% in the April 2026 quarter is entirely purchased.
  • Every platform line grows faster than its own market: SASE ARR +40% against a Dell'Oro SASE market at +21%; software firewall ARR +25% against a software and SaaS-delivered network security market Dell'Oro puts near 15% compound to 2029; XSIAM ARR about +100% against SIEM at 10-12%; hardware NGFW bookings about +40% against a firewall market growing at single digits.
  • Roughly $24.0bn of purchase consideration moved the like-for-like growth rate of the whole enterprise by about two and a half points.
  • The growth rate inside the slide is more defensible than the level: $59bn to $80bn over three years is 10.6% a year, and Dell'Oro forecasts firewalls at nearly double digits and SSE at 16% over 2025-2030.
  • Reaching $20bn from the FY2026 guided exit of $8.90-8.95bn requires 22.4% compound growth for four years and average net new NGS ARR near $2.8bn a year, against organic net new NGS ARR of $370m in Q3 FY2026 and roughly $1.4bn across the four quarters to 2026-04-30.
  • The platformization half is milder: 4,000 from about 2,280 needs roughly 101 net additions a quarter for 17 quarters against 110 delivered in Q3 FY2026 and a disclosed range of about 60 to 150 across the nine quarters PANW has given.
  • Compounding that at CyberArk's own roughly 30% subscription-ARR growth for four years gives about $4.7bn against a $5bn raise, so the acquired books account for the raise and the organic ambition did not move.
technology
  • One technical asset is hard to copy, and it is a deployment position: about one million firewalls in the field, 125 million sensors and 17 petabytes of daily telemetry, all of it inline in customer traffic (Q3 FY2026 call).
  • That advantage is real and it is the one that pays, because it converts a hardware refresh into a subscription annuity. It is delivered through software packaging, and no physics or ecosystem barrier protects it.
  • The economic hook is that a SIEM is priced on data ingested, so a platform that pre-analyses at ingest and routes cheaply changes the customer's cost curve.
value-chain
  • Holding the distributor band at 7-11% and varying the reseller margin between 10% and 25% puts PANW's own capture at $0.67 to $0.84 of the end-customer dollar.

Palo Alto Networks sells enterprise security in three commercial forms, all of them subscriptions attached to something already deployed.

Company

earnings callsupports
A key differentiator for our hardware portfolio remains the strength of our subscription attach, illustrating how customers are standardizing the security stack on our platform. Within our installed base, we currently average more than 4 subscriptions per device. Our innovation engine continues to expand this opportunity. We now provide 11 advanced subscriptions, including our next-generation trust security, which utilizes CyberArk's Certificate Management to address emerging compliance standards for shorter certificate life spans.
— Nikesh Arora, Chairman & CEO, Palo Alto Networks, Inc., Q3 2026 Earnings Call, 2026-06-02

In Q3, we surpassed $200 million in ARR with a leading frontier AI lab that relies on us for observability across its most demanding training and inference clusters. We expect that to continue to grow again next quarter as they complete their migration to Chronosphere. ... The world's leading AI natives, including 2 of the top 5 frontier labs, have adopted Chronosphere, validating our ability to provide observability at AI scale.
— Nikesh Arora, Chairman & CEO, Palo Alto Networks, Inc., Q3 2026 Earnings Call, 2026-06-02
earnings callqualifies
Palo Alto Networks remains the fastest-growing provider in the SASE market. In Q3, SASE ARR reached $1.6 billion, growing 40% year-over-year as customers prioritize unified protection across hybrid workforces and AI applications. Competitive momentum remains high, with nearly 50 displacement wins totaling $200 million in contract value year-to-date. Secure browser also achieved a major milestone, scaling to 11 million licenses, a fourfold increase that cements the status as a critical control point for the AI enterprise.
— Nikesh Arora, Chairman & CEO, Palo Alto Networks, Inc., Q3 2026 Earnings Call, 2026-06-02
filingsupports ✦ best
Our subscription and support revenue grew to $2.4 billion, or 80.2% of total revenue, for the third quarter of fiscal 2026, representing year-over-year growth of 31%. ... When customers purchase our physical, virtual, or container firewalls, or certain cloud offerings, they typically purchase support in order to receive ongoing security updates, upgrades, bug fixes, and repairs. In addition to the subscriptions purchased with these firewalls, customers may also purchase other subscriptions on a per-user, per-endpoint, or capacity-based basis.
— PANW Form 10-Q for the quarterly period ended April 30, 2026 (fiscal Q3 2026), 2026-04-30
filingsupports
Product revenue is derived from sales of hardware products, primarily our ML-Powered Next-Generation Firewall, and software licenses, including SD-WAN, VM-Series, Panorama, and certain identity security offerings. Our hardware products and software licenses include a broad set of built-in networking and security features and functionalities. We recognize product revenue at the time of hardware shipment or delivery of software license. As a percentage of product revenue, we expect our revenue from software licenses to vary from quarter to quarter and increase over the long term as we improve features and capabilities of our on-premise software, renew our software license contracts, and expand our installed end-customer base.
— PANW Form 10-Q for the quarterly period ended April 30, 2026 (fiscal Q3 2026), 2026-04-30

earnings callcontradicts
Most investors have equated our product revenue with hardware. However, given the drivers I have mentioned here, this has been rapidly shifting. -- software now contributes 30% of our product revenue. This is up from about 10% 3 years ago. We expect this trend to continue.
— Nikesh Arora, Chairman & CEO, Palo Alto Networks, Inc., Q3 2023 Earnings Call, 2023-05-23
earnings callqualifies
Yes, Adam, I think there are 2 parts to it. One is, as you will appreciate, we highlighted that software has become 30% of our product revenue. So we -- when you book a hardware firewall, you get a dollar for dollar for revenue. In software, you don't get a dollar for dollar for revenue there is some part of an amortized value we get from our software firewalls and some part of our SD-WAN, which becomes part of our product revenue. So we have to run harder on billings to be able to deliver product revenue in the context of software.
— Nikesh Arora, Chairman & CEO, Palo Alto Networks, Inc., Q3 2023 Earnings Call, 2023-05-23

At the disclosed attach of more than four subscriptions per installed device, the annual recurring bill at list is 4 x 27% + 19% = 127% of the box price, so across a five-year deployment the customer pays roughly 6.4x the appliance price in subscriptions and support and the box is about 14% of five-year spend.

filingqualifies
We believe that dollar-based net retention rate is an indicator to measure the long-term value of our customer relationships because it is driven by our ability to retain and expand the recurring revenue generated from our existing customers. Our dollar-based net retention rate compares the recurring revenue from a set of customers against the same metric for the prior 12-month period on a trailing basis. Because our customers have repeat buying patterns and the average term of our contracts is more than 12 months, we measure this metric over a set of customers who were with us as of the last day of the same reporting period in the prior fiscal year. For the trailing 12 months ended July 31, 2025 and 2024, the dollar-based net retention rate was 114% and 116%, respectively.
